CHEATSHEET: predicting 50-gene expression (log1p counts) from 224x224 H&E patches (0.5 um/px, ~112 um) of human skin melanoma
====================================================================================================================
Source: one Xenium section of cutaneous melanoma (nodular tumour under/through epidermis), aggregated to spots on a 200-px grid
(patch 224 px, so neighbouring patches overlap slightly). 1741 spots. Values are log(1+count) per spot.

PART 1. THE BIG PICTURE (read this even if you cannot run code)
---------------------------------------------------------------
Expression in this panel is dominated by THREE programs (PCA: PC1 57% of variance, PC2 20%):

 (A) KERATINOCYTE / EPIDERMIS program  (all strongly co-vary, r~0.8-0.9 with each other):
     AQP3 COL17A1 DSP KRT5 KRT16 KRT17 KRTDAP MAL2 S100A14 SBSN SERPINB5 TP63 MIR205HG LY6D FGFBP1 SLPI DSC1
     AHNAK2 CAPNS2 CRABP2 DEFB1 DMKN RHOV ACER1 KRT2 SERPINB2 LAMB3(weaker)
     - Basal/lower layers favour: KRT5, TP63, COL17A1, MIR205HG, LY6D, FGFBP1, AQP3, LAMB3, SERPINB5
     - Upper (spinous/granular, flattened, eosinophilic) layers favour: KRTDAP, SBSN, SLPI, DSC1, KRT2, SERPINB2, DMKN, DEFB1, ACER1
     High (5-8) ONLY where the patch contains real stratified squamous epithelium (epidermis / follicle / adnexal epithelium).
     In melanoma nests these genes are low (~0.5-1.7) but rarely exactly 0 (spillover).

 (B) MELANOMA TUMOUR + STROMA program: TYRP1, VIM, POSTN, MGP, HAPLN1 (+ moderately CRABP2, LAMB3, DMKN, LYZ)
     The tumour cells in this section themselves carry high TYRP1 (~5), VIM (~6.3), POSTN (~6.5), MGP (~6), HAPLN1 (~3.6).
     These are the most predictable genes (cross-validated r ~0.85-0.91 from simple image features).
     They are LOW in epidermis (TYRP1~2, VIM~2.5, POSTN~1.8, MGP~1.3) and VERY LOW in empty/acellular patches.

 (C) IMMUNE / VASCULAR program (weaker, noisier, small counts): LYZ (macrophage, highest level), BIRC3, CD52, WDFY4, LCK,
     GZMK, ITM2A, IDO1, CCL19, CCL22, CD1B, IL1B, HBD, SELE, TFF3, MYH11, VWF, MMRN1 (VWF/MMRN1/SELE/TFF3 = endothelium/lymphatics;
     MYH11 = smooth muscle/pericytes). These rise where small dark round lymphocytes / histiocytes infiltrate between tumour nests,
     in stroma bands, and around vessels. Most are 0-2 in log space.

Tissue classes seen in this section (k-means on expression, 6 classes), with what they look like:

  class 3: TUMOUR NEST (49% of spots). Sheets/nests of large epithelioid melanoma cells: big round-oval vesicular nuclei with prominent nucleoli, moderate pale-pink/lilac cytoplasm, dense cellularity, little white space, few lymphocytes. Fine fibrovascular septa may cross. Purple-lilac overall colour (less saturated pink than epidermis).
  class 0: TUMOUR + STROMA/IMMUNE/VESSELS (16%). Tumour nests interrupted by fibrous septa, loose oedematous/myxoid stroma, vessels (open lumens, RBCs), and infiltrating small dark lymphocytes / histiocytes; spindle cells. Highest LYZ, CD52, WDFY4, LCK, GZMK, ITM2A, VWF, MMRN1, BIRC3.
  class 2: ACELLULAR / BACKGROUND / LOOSE (17%). Mostly white glass, detached keratin flakes, serum/fibrin, thin fibrous strands, hyalinised pink collagen, blurry tissue edges, few nuclei. EVERYTHING LOW (most genes <1); KRT16/KRT17/DSP/SBSN ~1-2 if keratin/epithelial fragments are present; VIM/POSTN/MGP ~1.5.
  class 1: FULL EPIDERMIS / SQUAMOUS EPITHELIUM (8%). Stratified squamous keratinocytes: cohesive polygonal cells with abundant pink cytoplasm, intercellular bridges, evenly spaced smaller oval nuclei, basal palisade; may include rete ridges, follicle epithelium. All keratinocyte genes very high (5-7.5). TYRP1/VIM/POSTN still moderately high (melanocytes / tumour junctional component / dermis adjacent).
  class 4: UPPER EPIDERMIS / KERATINISING (3%). Bright saturated pink, flattened cells in parallel lamellae, few elongated or pyknotic nuclei, granular/cornified layers, compact keratin pearls/lamellae, wavy parallel texture. Highest KRTDAP, SBSN, SLPI, SERPINB2, DMKN; KRT5/TP63/COL17A1 lower than full epidermis; tumour/immune genes LOW (TYRP1~1.9, VIM~2.5, MGP~1.3).
  class 5: EPIDERMIS-TUMOUR INTERFACE / BASAL EPITHELIUM (7%). Mixture: basal keratinocyte layer or thin epithelium abutting tumour or inflamed dermis, junctional zone. Keratinocyte genes high (basal ones highest: KRT5 ~6.3, COL17A1 ~5.3, TP63 ~4.1) AND tumour genes high (TYRP1~5, VIM~5.8, POSTN~5), plus immune moderate.

PRACTICAL VISUAL RULES
- First decide: how much of the patch is (i) white/empty, (ii) stratified squamous epithelium, (iii) melanoma cells, (iv) stroma/immune/vessel.
  Predictions are roughly the area-weighted mixture of class templates (spots are aggregates, so mixing is linear in counts,
  approximately linear in log space for moderate values).
- White fraction strongly scales everything down. A patch that is >70% white with a thin strip of fibrous tissue -> class c2 template.
  A patch half white, half tumour -> about halfway between c2 and c3 for tumour genes (e.g. VIM ~4.5-5.5, POSTN ~4.5-5.5).
- Epidermis vs tumour: epidermis = brighter/more saturated pink cytoplasm, cells cohesive with sharp borders, nuclei smaller and
  evenly spaced, layering/orientation (parallel flattening toward surface); tumour = lilac/purple, larger nuclei with nucleoli,
  crowded, pleomorphic, nests separated by thin septa. Keratin genes jump by ~4-5 log units between them: getting this right matters most.
- Within epidermis: basal/thick living epidermis -> KRT5, TP63, COL17A1, MIR205HG, AQP3 ~5-7; flattened/cornifying pink layers ->
  KRTDAP, SBSN, SLPI ~5-6 and KRT5/TP63 lower (~2-4). Pure stratum corneum flakes (anucleate pink wisps) -> low everything (c2).
- Smooth parallel pink fibres with few elongated nuclei (dense collagen/hyalinised dermis, fibrous capsule) -> c2/c4-like: low tumour genes;
  if those fibres are cellular wavy keratinised lamellae -> c4.
- Many small dark round lymphocyte nuclei between tumour cells -> raise CD52, LCK, GZMK, WDFY4, ITM2A, BIRC3, LYZ, IDO1 by ~1 log unit
  over c3 template (to c0 levels or higher; LYZ up to 5-6, CD52 up to 3-4).
- Open vessel lumens / RBCs / endothelium -> VWF 2.5-4, MMRN1 1-3, SELE ~1, TFF3 ~1, MYH11 ~1.
- Melanin pigment (brown granules) was rare here; TYRP1 is high in essentially all tumour (~5-5.5) regardless of visible pigment.
- Loose myxoid/oedematous tumour stroma still had high MGP/POSTN/VIM/HAPLN1 (~5-6).
- Rarely predict exact 0 for a gene unless the patch is nearly empty; under squared error, hedge toward the class mean.

  (white=fraction white background; sat_t=mean colour saturation (max-min RGB) of tissue pixels: high in pink epidermis;
   grad1=mean edge strength; nn=count of nuclei; Hq75=75th pct hematoxylin density; Eq50=median eosin density;
   coh1=orientation coherence (parallel layered texture); ndist=mean distance of tissue pixels to nearest nucleus)

PART 2. QUANTITATIVE MODEL YOU CAN REBUILD (if you can run Python with numpy/scipy/PIL; sklearn optional)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Below: (a) exact feature-extraction code, (b) the full training table for all 1741 spots: 43 image features + 50 genes,
each value encoded as ONE character. Decode, train a model, apply to new patches.
Spatially blocked 5-fold CV on this section with these features:
  ExtraTrees/RandomForest (500 trees, min_samples_leaf=3, max_features=0.3): mean per-gene Pearson r = 0.67, overall R2 = 0.54
  kNN (k=10-20, quantile-scaled features): r = 0.61 ; Ridge: r = 0.63
  Best genes: POSTN/VIM 0.91, MGP 0.86, TYRP1 0.85, LYZ 0.75, HAPLN1 0.72; keratinocyte genes ~0.6-0.7; weakest: MMRN1, CCL19, CD1B, TFF3, IL1B ~0.35-0.45.
Recommended: ExtraTreesRegressor (multi-output) on all 43 features; optionally average with your own visual judgement (Part 1),
especially for epidermis-vs-tumour calls, which the colour/texture features sometimes confuse.
If the new images have a different stain intensity, consider matching colour: the features below assume this section's staining
(median tissue saturation ~80, median H 75th pct ~0.3). Feature quantile-scaling per new dataset can reduce stain shift.

(a) FEATURE CODE (Python). feats(path) returns a dict of 43 features in this exact key order.
```python
import numpy as np, pandas as pd
from PIL import Image
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
M=np.array([[0.65,0.70,0.29],[0.07,0.99,0.11],[0.27,0.57,0.78]]);M=M/np.linalg.norm(M,axis=1,keepdims=True);Minv=np.linalg.inv(M)
def feats(path):
    a=np.asarray(Image.open(path).convert('RGB')).astype(np.float64)
    od=-np.log((a+1)/256.)
    C=od.reshape(-1,3)@Minv; H=C[:,0].reshape(224,224); E=C[:,1].reshape(224,224)
    gray=a.mean(2); sat=a.max(2)-a.min(2)
    white=(gray>215)&(sat<25); t=~white; tf=t.mean()
    f={}
    f['white']=white.mean()
    for nm,ch in (('H',H),('E',E)):
        for q in (10,25,50,75,90,99): f[f'{nm}q{q}']=np.percentile(ch,q)
        f[nm+'t']=ch[t].mean() if t.sum()>50 else 0
    for i,c in enumerate('RGB'):
        f[c+'m']=a[...,i].mean(); f[c+'s']=a[...,i].std()
    f['satm']=sat.mean(); f['sat_t']=sat[t].mean() if t.sum()>50 else 0
    # nuclei
    nuc=ndi.binary_opening(H>0.55,iterations=1)
    lab,n=ndi.label(nuc)
    if n:
        idx=range(1,n+1)
        sz=np.asarray(ndi.sum(np.ones_like(H),lab,idx)); keep=sz>=30
        Hmean=np.asarray(ndi.mean(H,lab,idx))
        sl=ndi.find_objects(lab)
        el=np.array([max(s_[0].stop-s_[0].start,s_[1].stop-s_[1].start)/max(1,min(s_[0].stop-s_[0].start,s_[1].stop-s_[1].start)) for s_ in sl])
    else: sz=np.array([0.]);keep=np.array([False]);Hmean=np.array([0.]);el=np.array([1.])
    f['nucfrac']=nuc.mean(); f['nn']=keep.sum(); f['nn_t']=keep.sum()/max(tf,0.05)
    f['nsz50']=np.median(sz[keep]) if keep.any() else 0
    f['nsz90']=np.percentile(sz[keep],90) if keep.any() else 0
    f['nH']=Hmean[keep].mean() if keep.any() else 0
    f['nel']=np.median(el[keep]) if keep.any() else 1
    f['nel_frac']=(el[keep]>2.5).mean() if keep.any() else 0
    f['nsmall']=((sz>=30)&(sz<80)).sum()/max(tf,0.05)
    # nuclear spacing: distance transform of non-nuclear tissue
    if keep.any():
        dt=ndi.distance_transform_edt(~nuc); f['ndist']=dt[t].mean() if t.sum()>50 else 50
    else: f['ndist']=50
    # texture multi-scale
    for k,sc in enumerate((1,2,4)):
        g=gray[::sc,::sc] if sc>1 else gray
        g=ndi.uniform_filter(gray,sc)[::sc,::sc]
        gm=np.hypot(ndi.sobel(g,0),ndi.sobel(g,1)); f[f'grad{sc}']=gm.mean()
        # orientation coherence
        gx=ndi.sobel(g,1);gy=ndi.sobel(g,0)
        Jxx=(gx*gx).mean();Jyy=(gy*gy).mean();Jxy=(gx*gy).mean()
        f[f'coh{sc}']=np.sqrt((Jxx-Jyy)**2+4*Jxy**2)/(Jxx+Jyy+1e-9)
    # E texture
    f['Egrad']=np.hypot(ndi.sobel(E,0),ndi.sobel(E,1)).mean()
    f['lap']=np.abs(ndi.laplace(ndi.gaussian_filter(gray,1))).mean()
    # blood
    f['rbc']=((a[...,0]>150)&(a[...,1]<90)&(a[...,2]<140)&(E>0.8)).mean()
    f['pale']=((gray>180)&(gray<=215)).mean()
    return f
```

(b) DECODING. Alphabet (90 symbols, index = position):
ALPH = "0123456789ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz!#$%&()*+,-./:;<=>?@[]^_{|}~"
Feature j: value = lo_j + idx/89 * (hi_j - lo_j)   (values were clipped to [lo_j,hi_j] before encoding)
Gene: value = idx/10   (log1p expression rounded to 0.1)
Each data row = 43 feature chars, then a space, then 50 gene chars. Rows are between the lines BEGIN_DATA / END_DATA.
Feature order, lo, hi:

Decoder:
```python
import numpy as np
def load_table(path='cheatsheet.txt'):
    txt=open(path,encoding='utf-8').read().splitlines()
    ALPH=[l for l in txt if l.startswith('ALPH = ')][0].split('"')[1]
    idx={c:i for i,c in enumerate(ALPH)}
    fl=[l.split() for l in txt if l.startswith('F ')]
    names=[f[1] for f in fl]; lo=np.array([float(f[2]) for f in fl]); hi=np.array([float(f[3]) for f in fl])
    genes=[l for l in txt if l.startswith('Gene order: ')][0].split()[2:]
    a=txt.index('BEGIN_DATA')+1; b=txt.index('END_DATA')
    X=[];Y=[]
    for l in txt[a:b]:
        f,g=l.split(' ')
        X.append([idx[c] for c in f]); Y.append([idx[c] for c in g])
    X=lo+np.array(X)/89.*(hi-lo); Y=np.array(Y)/10.
    return names,X,genes,Y
# names,X,genes,Y = load_table()
# from sklearn.ensemble import ExtraTreesRegressor
# m=ExtraTreesRegressor(500,min_samples_leaf=3,max_features=0.3,n_jobs=-1,random_state=0).fit(X,Y)
# x_new=np.array([[feats(p)[k] for k in names] for p in new_paths]); x_new=np.clip(x_new,lo,hi)  # clip like training
# pred=m.predict(x_new)
```
Without sklearn: standardise features (subtract median, divide by IQR), take the 15 nearest training rows (Euclidean), average their genes.
